Theorem 218.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For convergence analysis pairs (A,B) in systematics theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 961 and the invariant I(A,B) = 993 mod 4.

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_961(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 961. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^993(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 218.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: For any systematics module M satisfying the structural decomposition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 557. The projective resolution has length at most 236 with Betti numbers bounded by 5.

Proof: We apply the structural decomposition principle to the systematics decomposition. By minimality, the element a^557 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 236-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
